---
name: using-superpowers
description: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring Skill tool invocation before ANY response including clarifying questions
---

<EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>
If you think there is even a 1% chance a skill might apply to what you are doing, you ABSOLUTELY MUST read the skill.

IF A SKILL APPLIES TO YOUR TASK, YOU DO NOT HAVE A CHOICE. YOU MUST USE IT.

This is not negotiable. This is not optional. You cannot rationalize your way out of this.
</EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>

# Using Skills

## The Rule

**Check for skills BEFORE ANY RESPONSE.** This includes clarifying questions. Even 1% chance means invoke the Skill tool first.

```dot
digraph skill_flow {
    "User message received" [shape=doublecircle];
    "Might any skill apply?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke Skill tool" [shape=box];
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" [shape=box];
    "Has checklist?" [shape=diamond];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [shape=box];
    "Follow skill exactly" [shape=box];
    "Respond (including clarifications)" [shape=doublecircle];

    "User message received" -> "Might any skill apply?";
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Invoke Skill tool" [label="yes, even 1%"];
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Respond (including clarifications)" [label="definitely not"];
    "Invoke Skill tool" -> "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'";
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" -> "Has checklist?";
    "Has checklist?" -> "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [label="yes"];
    "Has checklist?" -> "Follow skill exactly" [label="no"];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" -> "Follow skill exactly";
}
```

## Red Flags

These thoughts mean STOP—you're rationalizing:

| Thought | Reality |
|---------|---------|
| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |

## Skill Priority

When multiple skills could apply, use this order:

1. **Process skills first** (brainstorming, debugging) - these determine HOW to approach the task
2. **Implementation skills second** (frontend-design, mcp-builder) - these guide execution

"Let's build X" → brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
"Fix this bug" → debugging first, then domain-specific skills.

## Skill Types

**Rigid** (TDD, debugging): Follow exactly. Don't adapt away discipline.

**Flexible** (patterns): Adapt principles to context.

The skill itself tells you which.

## User Instructions

Instructions say WHAT, not HOW. "Add X" or "Fix Y" doesn't mean skip workflows.
---
name: code-reviewer
description: Comprehensive code review skill for TypeScript, JavaScript, Python, Swift, Kotlin, Go. # Code Reviewer

Complete toolkit for code reviewer with modern tools and best practices.

## Quick Start

### Main Capabilities

This skill provides three core capabilities through automated scripts:

```bash
# Script 1: Pr Analyzer
python scripts/pr_analyzer.py [options]

# Script 2: Code Quality Checker
python scripts/code_quality_checker.py [options]

# Script 3: Review Report Generator
python scripts/review_report_generator.py [options]
```

## Core Capabilities

### 1. Pr Analyzer

Automated tool for pr analyzer tasks.

**Features:**
- Automated scaffolding
- Best practices built-in
- Configurable templates
- Quality checks

**Usage:**
```bash
python scripts/pr_analyzer.py <project-path> [options]
```

### 2. Code Quality Checker

Comprehensive analysis and optimization tool.

**Features:**
- Deep analysis
- Performance metrics
- Recommendations
- Automated fixes

**Usage:**
```bash
python scripts/code_quality_checker.py <target-path> [--verbose]
```

### 3. Review Report Generator

Advanced tooling for specialized tasks.

**Features:**
- Expert-level automation
- Custom configurations
- Integration ready
- Production-grade output

**Usage:**
```bash
python scripts/review_report_generator.py [arguments] [options]
```

- Monitor in production

### Security
- Validate all inputs
- Use parameterized queries
- Implement proper authentication
- Keep dependencies updated

### Maintainability
- Write clear code
- Use consistent naming
- Add helpful comments
- Keep it simple

# Supabase

## Core Principles

**1. Supabase changes frequently — verify against changelog and current docs before implementing.**
Do not rely on training data for Supabase features. Function signatures, config.toml settings, and API conventions change between versions.

First, fetch `https://supabase.com/changelog.md` (a lightweight summary index — not a heavy pull), scan for `breaking-change` tags relevant to your task, and follow the linked page for any that apply. Then look up the relevant topic using the documentation access methods below.

**2. Verify your work.**
After implementing any fix, run a test query to confirm the change works. A fix without verification is incomplete.

**3. Recover from errors, don't loop.**
If an approach fails after 2-3 attempts, stop and reconsider. Try a different method, check documentation, inspect the error more carefully, and review relevant logs when available. Supabase issues are not always solved by retrying the same command, and the answer is not always in the logs, but logs are often worth checking before proceeding.

**4. Exposing tables to the Data API:** Depending on the user's [Data API settings](https://supabase.com/dashboard/project/<ref>/integrations/data_api/settings), newly created tables may not be automatically exposed via the Data (REST) API. If this is the case, `anon` and `authenticated` roles will need to be explicitly granted access.

> Note that this is separate from RLS, which controls which _rows_ are visible once a table is accessible, not whether the table is accessible at all.

When a user reports a SQL-created table is unexpectedly inaccessible, check their Data API settings and whether the roles have been granted access via explicit `GRANT` SQL. When granting public (`anon`/`authenticated`) access, always enable RLS too. See [Exposing a Table to the Data API](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/api/securing-your-api.md) for the full setup workflow.

**5. RLS in exposed schemas.**
Enable RLS on every table in any exposed schema, which includes `public` by default. This is critical in Supabase because tables in exposed schemas can be reachable through the Data API when the `anon`/`authenticated` roles have access (see [Exposing a Table to the Data API](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/api/securing-your-api.md)). For private schemas, prefer RLS as defense in depth. After enabling RLS, create policies that match the actual access model rather than defaulting every table to the same `auth.uid()` pattern.

**6. Security checklist.**
When working on any Supabase task that touches auth, RLS, views, storage, or user data, run through this checklist. These are Supabase-specific security traps that silently create vulnerabilities:

- **Auth and session security**
  - **Never use `user_metadata` claims in JWT-based authorization decisions.** In Supabase, `raw_user_meta_data` is user-editable and can appear in `auth.jwt()`, so it is unsafe for RLS policies or any other authorization logic. Store authorization data in `raw_app_meta_data` / `app_metadata` instead.
  - **Deleting a user does not invalidate existing access tokens.** Sign out or revoke sessions first, keep JWT expiry short for sensitive apps, and for strict guarantees validate `session_id` against `auth.sessions` on sensitive operations.
  - **If you use `app_metadata` or `auth.jwt()` for authorization, remember JWT claims are not always fresh until the user's token is refreshed.**

- **API key and client exposure**
  - **Never expose the `service_role` or secret key in public clients.** Prefer publishable keys for frontend code. Legacy `anon` keys are only for compatibility. In Next.js, any `NEXT_PUBLIC_` env var is sent to the browser.

- **RLS, views, and privileged database code**
  - **Views bypass RLS by default.** In Postgres 15 and above, use `CREATE VIEW ... WITH (security_invoker = true)`. In older versions of Postgres, protect your views by revoking access from the `anon` and `authenticated` roles, or by putting them in an unexposed schema.
  - **UPDATE requires a SELECT policy.** In Postgres RLS, an UPDATE needs to first SELECT the row. Without a SELECT policy, updates silently return 0 rows — no error, just no change.
  - **`auth.role()` is deprecated — use the `TO` clause instead.** Supabase has deprecated `auth.role()` in favour of specifying the target role directly on the policy with `TO authenticated` or `TO anon`. Beyond deprecation, `auth.role() = 'authenticated'` breaks silently when anonymous sign-ins are enabled, because anonymous users carry the `authenticated` Postgres role and pass the check regardless of whether the user is genuinely signed in.
    ```sql
    -- Deprecated (do not use)
    create policy "example" on table_name for select
    using ( auth.role() = 'authenticated' );
    ```
  - **`TO authenticated` alone is authentication without authorization (BOLA / IDOR).** Using `TO authenticated` only checks the role — it does not restrict which rows a user can access. The correct pattern combines `TO authenticated` with an ownership predicate in `USING`:
    ```sql
    create policy "example" on table_name for select
    to authenticated
    using ( (select auth.uid()) = user_id );
    ```
  - **UPDATE policies require both `USING` and `WITH CHECK`.** Without `WITH CHECK`, a user can reassign a row's `user_id` to another user:
    ```sql
    create policy "example" on table_name for update
    to authenticated
    using ( (select auth.uid()) = user_id )
    with check ( (select auth.uid()) = user_id );
    ```
  - **`SECURITY DEFINER` functions bypass RLS.** A `SECURITY DEFINER` function runs with its creator's privileges — typically a role with `bypassrls` (e.g., `postgres`). Never add `SECURITY DEFINER` to resolve a permission error; it silently removes access control without fixing the underlying cause. Prefer `SECURITY INVOKER`.
  - **`SECURITY DEFINER` functions in `public` are callable by all roles.** Postgres grants `EXECUTE` to `PUBLIC` by default for every new function, so any `SECURITY DEFINER` function in `public` is a public API endpoint callable by `anon` and `authenticated` (which inherit from `PUBLIC`) without any additional grant. When `SECURITY DEFINER` is genuinely needed (e.g., bypassing RLS on an internal lookup table), keep the function in a non-exposed schema, always include an `auth.uid()` check in the function body, and run `supabase db advisors` after making changes.

- **Storage access control**
  - **Storage upsert requires INSERT + SELECT + UPDATE.** Granting only INSERT allows new uploads but file replacement (upsert) silently fails. You need all three.

- **Dependency and supply-chain security**
  - **Always pin package versions and commit lockfiles** when installing Supabase packages (`supabase-js`, `@supabase/ssr`, `supabase-py`, etc.). See the [npm security guide](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/security/npm-security.md) for the full checklist.

For any security concern not covered above, fetch the Supabase product security index: `https://supabase.com/docs/guides/security/product-security.md`

## Supabase CLI

Always discover commands via `--help` — never guess. The CLI structure changes between versions.

```bash
supabase --help                    # All top-level commands
supabase <group> --help            # Subcommands (e.g., supabase db --help)
supabase <group> <command> --help  # Flags for a specific command
```

**Supabase CLI Known gotchas:**

- `supabase db query` requires **CLI v2.79.0+** → use MCP `execute_sql` or `psql` as fallback
- `supabase db advisors` requires **CLI v2.81.3+** → use MCP `get_advisors` as fallback
- In imperative migration projects, create new hand-authored migration files with `supabase migration new <name>` first. Never invent a migration filename or rely on memory for the expected format. Declarative schema projects generate migrations from `supabase/schemas/`; see "Making and Committing Schema Changes" below.

**Version check and upgrade:** Run `supabase --version` to check. For CLI changelogs and version-specific features, consult the [CLI documentation](https://supabase.com/docs/reference/cli/introduction) or [GitHub releases](https://github.com/supabase/cli/releases).

## Supabase MCP Server

For setup instructions, server URL, and configuration, see the [MCP setup guide](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/getting-started/mcp).

**Troubleshooting connection issues** — follow these steps in order:

1. **Check if the server is reachable:**
   `curl -so /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" https://mcp.supabase.com/mcp`
   A `401` is expected (no token) and means the server is up. Timeout or "connection refused" means it may be down.

2. **Check `.mcp.json` configuration:**
   Verify the project root has a valid `.mcp.json` with the correct server URL. If missing, create one pointing to `https://mcp.supabase.com/mcp`.

3. **Authenticate the MCP server:**
   If the server is reachable and `.mcp.json` is correct but tools aren't visible, the user needs to authenticate. The Supabase MCP server uses OAuth 2.1 — tell the user to trigger the auth flow in their agent, complete it in the browser, and reload the session.

## Supabase Documentation

Before implementing any Supabase feature, find the relevant documentation. Use these methods in priority order:

1. **MCP `search_docs` tool** (preferred — returns relevant snippets directly)
2. **Fetch docs pages as markdown** — any docs page can be fetched by appending `.md` to the URL path.
3. **Web search** for Supabase-specific topics when you don't know which page to look at.

## Making and Committing Schema Changes

First decide which schema workflow the project uses.

### Option A: Declarative schemas

Use this when `supabase/schemas/` exists or `config.toml` sets `schema_paths`. Edit the desired schema state in those files, then generate and review the migration. Do not start by hand-writing a migration. See the [Declarative database schemas guide](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/local-development/declarative-database-schemas).

### Option B: Imperative migrations

Use this when the project does not use declarative schemas.

**To make schema changes, use `execute_sql` (MCP) or `supabase db query` (CLI).** These run SQL directly on the database without creating migration history entries, so you can iterate freely and generate a clean migration when ready.

Do NOT use `apply_migration` to change a local database schema — it writes a migration history entry on every call, which means you can't iterate, and `supabase db diff` / `supabase db pull` will produce empty or conflicting diffs. If you use it, you'll be stuck with whatever SQL you passed on the first try.

**When ready to commit** your changes to a migration file:

1. **Run advisors** → `supabase db advisors` (CLI v2.81.3+) or MCP `get_advisors`. Fix any issues.
2. **Review the Security Checklist above** if your changes involve views, functions, triggers, or storage.
3. **Generate the migration** → `supabase db pull <descriptive-name> --local --yes`
4. **Verify** → `supabase migration list --local`

## How each capability type is invoked

| Type | Where it lives | How to invoke |
|------|----------------|---------------|
| **Skill** | `~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` (user) · plugin bundles · harness built-ins | `/<name>` or auto-triggered by description; via `Skill` tool |
| **Agent** (subagent) | `~/.claude/agents/<name>.md` (user) · plugin bundles | `Agent` tool with `subagent_type: <name>`, or say "use the `<name>` agent" |
| **Command** | `~/.claude/commands/<name>.md` (user) | `/<name>` (unified with skills) |
| **Plugin** | `~/.claude/plugins/cache/<marketplace>/<plugin>/` | bundles skills + agents + commands + hooks + MCP under a namespace |
| **MCP tool** | server process; config in `~/.claude.json` or plugin | `mcp__<server>__<tool>` (deferred → load via `ToolSearch` first) |

Archived / removed capabilities are backed up in `~/Documents/claude/{skills,agents,commands}/` — copy a folder back to `~/.claude/…` to reinstall.

echo "MCP (global):"; python3 -c "import json,os;print('\n'.join(json.load(open(os.path.expanduser('~/.claude.json'))).get('mcpServers',{})))"

+// ── Hard-delete eligibility: total operational/financial references ───────
+// Mirrors public.user_deletion_profile(uuid) (7 categories) exactly. That RPC
+// is SECDEF-gated on auth.uid(), which is NULL under the service-role client,
+// so it can't be called here — this is the same count set run directly.
+async function countUserReferences(
+  supabaseClient: any,
+  userId: string,
+): Promise<{ counts: Record<string, number>; total: number }> {
+  const count = async (table: string, build: (q: any) => any): Promise<number> => {
+    const { count: c, error } = await build(supabaseClient.from(table).select("id", { count: "exact", head: true }));
+    if (error) throw error;
+    return c ?? 0;
+  };
+
+  const [
+    commandes,
+    sellerPayments, mediabuyerPayments, courierPaymentNotes,
+    colis,
+    companyDeliveryNotes, companyReturnNotes, courierDeliveryNotes, courierReturnNotes,
+    campaigns, campaignDailyEntries, adsHistory,
+    sellerDemands, mediabuyerDemands, parcelChangeDemands,
+    historique,
+  ] = await Promise.all([
+    count("orders", (q) => q.or(`seller_id.eq.${userId},confirmed_by_id.eq.${userId}`)),
+    count("seller_payments", (q) => q.eq("seller_id", userId)),
+    count("mediabuyer_payments", (q) => q.eq("mediabuyer_id", userId)),
+    count("courier_payment_notes", (q) => q.eq("courier_id", userId)),
+    count("courier_parcels", (q) => q.eq("courier_id", userId)),
+    count("company_delivery_notes", (q) => q.eq("created_by", userId)),
+    count("company_return_notes", (q) => q.eq("created_by", userId)),
+    count("courier_delivery_notes", (q) => q.or(`courier_id.eq.${userId},created_by.eq.${userId}`)),
+    count("courier_return_notes", (q) => q.or(`courier_id.eq.${userId},created_by.eq.${userId}`)),
+    count("campaigns", (q) => q.eq("created_by", userId)),
+    count("campaign_daily_entries", (q) => q.eq("created_by", userId)),
+    count("ads_history", (q) => q.eq("created_by", userId)),
+    count("seller_demands", (q) => q.eq("seller_id", userId)),
+    count("mediabuyer_demands", (q) => q.eq("mediabuyer_id", userId)),
+    count("parcel_change_demands", (q) => q.eq("created_by", userId)),
+    count("order_status_history", (q) => q.eq("changed_by", userId)),
+  ]);
+
+  const counts = {
+    commandes,
+    paiements: sellerPayments + mediabuyerPayments + courierPaymentNotes,
+    colis,
+    notes: companyDeliveryNotes + companyReturnNotes + courierDeliveryNotes + courierReturnNotes,
+    campagnes: campaigns + campaignDailyEntries + adsHistory,
+    demandes: sellerDemands + mediabuyerDemands + parcelChangeDemands,
+    historique,
+  };
+  const total = Object.values(counts).reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
+  return { counts, total };
+}

     1	-- TKT-12 (Notion Ref 10): user deletion — auth/public split fix + archive model.
     2	-- Load-bearing users are ARCHIVED (active=false + archived_at + GoTrue soft
     3	-- delete), never hard-deleted: orders.seller_id/confirmed_by_id and the note
     4	-- created_by FKs BLOCK deletion (the reported bug), and — worse — the payment
     5	-- ledgers (seller_payments, mediabuyer_payments) are ON DELETE CASCADE, so a
     6	-- "successful" hard delete would silently destroy money history.
     7	-- Hard delete stays available ONLY for users with zero operational references.
     8	-- All operational pickers already filter active=true → archived users vanish
     9	-- from rosters automatically; only the Users console shows them (Archivés).
    10	
    11	BEGIN;
    12	SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s';
    13	
    14	ALTER TABLE public.users ADD COLUMN archived_at timestamptz;
    15	
    16	-- One reference profile per user: powers the adaptive delete dialog (receipts)
    17	-- AND the server-side hard-delete eligibility guard. Counts only MEANINGFUL
    18	-- operational/financial references; account plumbing (push subscriptions,
    19	-- presences, GA/Meta access rows, credential requests, invitation stamps,
    20	-- email log) is deliberately excluded — it cascades or nulls harmlessly.
    21	CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.user_deletion_profile(p_user_id uuid)
    22	RETURNS jsonb
    23	LANGUAGE plpgsql
    24	STABLE
    25	SECURITY DEFINER
    26	SET search_path TO 'public', 'pg_catalog'
    27	AS $$
    28	DECLARE
    29	    v_me record;
    30	    v_target record;
    31	    v_counts jsonb;
    32	    v_total bigint;
    33	BEGIN
    34	    SELECT role::text AS role, "DigiCom Business" AS biz INTO v_me
    35	    FROM public.users WHERE id = auth.uid();
    36	    IF v_me.role IS NULL THEN
    37	        RAISE EXCEPTION 'Authentification requise.';
    38	    END IF;
    39	
    40	    SELECT role::text AS role, "DigiCom Business" AS biz INTO v_target
    41	    FROM public.users WHERE id = p_user_id;
    42	    IF v_target.role IS NULL THEN
    43	        RAISE EXCEPTION 'Utilisateur introuvable.';
    44	    END IF;
    45	
    46	    -- Caller must be the platform owner, or an admin-tier member of the
    47	    -- TARGET's business (mirrors the Users-console visibility rules).
    48	    IF v_me.role <> 'owner'
    49	       AND NOT (v_me.role IN ('business owner', 'super admin', 'admin')
    50	                AND v_me.biz = v_target.biz) THEN
    51	        RAISE EXCEPTION 'Accès refusé.';
    52	    END IF;
    53	
    54	    SELECT jsonb_build_object(
    55	        'commandes',    (SELECT count(*) FROM orders o WHERE o.seller_id = p_user_id OR o.confirmed_by_id = p_user_id),
    56	        'paiements',    (SELECT (SELECT count(*) FROM seller_payments sp WHERE sp.seller_id = p_user_id)
    57	                              + (SELECT count(*) FROM mediabuyer_payments mp WHERE mp.mediabuyer_id = p_user_id)
    58	                              + (SELECT count(*) FROM courier_payment_notes cpn WHERE cpn.courier_id = p_user_id)),
    59	        'colis',        (SELECT count(*) FROM courier_parcels cp WHERE cp.courier_id = p_user_id),
    60	        'notes',        (SELECT (SELECT count(*) FROM company_delivery_notes WHERE created_by = p_user_id)
    61	                              + (SELECT count(*) FROM company_return_notes WHERE created_by = p_user_id)
    62	                              + (SELECT count(*) FROM courier_delivery_notes WHERE courier_id = p_user_id OR created_by = p_user_id)
    63	                              + (SELECT count(*) FROM courier_return_notes WHERE courier_id = p_user_id OR created_by = p_user_id)),
    64	        'campagnes',    (SELECT (SELECT count(*) FROM campaigns WHERE created_by = p_user_id)
    65	                              + (SELECT count(*) FROM campaign_daily_entries WHERE created_by = p_user_id)
    66	                              + (SELECT count(*) FROM ads_history WHERE created_by = p_user_id)),
    67	        'demandes',     (SELECT (SELECT count(*) FROM seller_demands WHERE seller_id = p_user_id)
    68	                              + (SELECT count(*) FROM mediabuyer_demands WHERE mediabuyer_id = p_user_id)
    69	                              + (SELECT count(*) FROM parcel_change_demands WHERE created_by = p_user_id)),
    70	        'historique',   (SELECT count(*) FROM order_status_history WHERE changed_by = p_user_id)
    71	    ) INTO v_counts;
    72	
    73	    SELECT (v_counts->>'commandes')::bigint + (v_counts->>'paiements')::bigint
    74	         + (v_counts->>'colis')::bigint + (v_counts->>'notes')::bigint
    75	         + (v_counts->>'campagnes')::bigint + (v_counts->>'demandes')::bigint
    76	         + (v_counts->>'historique')::bigint
    77	    INTO v_total;
    78	
    79	    RETURN v_counts || jsonb_build_object(
    80	        'total', v_total,
    81	        'hard_deletable', v_total = 0
    82	    );
    83	END;
    84	$$;
    85	
    86	-- Box default ACL grants EXECUTE to everyone — restrict, then grant the one
    87	-- audience (the fn self-gates on caller identity above).
    88	REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.user_deletion_profile(uuid) FROM PUBLIC, anon;
    89	GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.user_deletion_profile(uuid) TO authenticated, service_role;
    90	
    91	COMMIT;

   125	    // Courier parcel handling — same keys the invite wizard sets.

   127	    courier_parcel_open: z.boolean().default(false),
   128	    courier_parcel_try_on: z.boolean().default(false),
   129	    // Mediabuyer integration scoping — which GA properties / Meta ad accounts they may see.
   130	    ga_access: z.object({

   147	// Reusable picker for a mediabuyer's GA / Meta access (mode none/all/specific + checkboxes).
   148	const IntegrationAccessField: React.FC<{
   149	    control: any;
   150	    name: 'ga_access' | 'meta_access';
   151	    title: string;
   152	    hint: string;
   153	    conns: any[] | undefined;
   154	    primaryKey: string;   // display label field
   155	    secondaryKey: string; // mono sub-label field (property_id / ad_account_id)
   156	    emptyText: string;
   157	}> = ({ control, name, title, hint, conns, primaryKey, secondaryKey, emptyText }) => {
   158	    const modes: Array<['none' | 'all' | 'specific', string]> = [['none', 'No access'], ['all', 'All'], ['specific', 'Specific']];
   159	    return (
   160	        <FormField
   161	            control={control}
   162	            name={name as any}
   163	            render={({ field }: any) => {
   164	                const access = field.value || { mode: 'none', ids: [] };
   165	                const setMode = (mode: 'none' | 'all' | 'specific') =>
   166	                    field.onChange({ mode, ids: mode === 'specific' ? (access.ids || []) : [] });
   167	                const toggle = (id: string) => {
   168	                    const cur: string[] = access.ids || [];
   169	                    field.onChange({ mode: 'specific', ids: cur.includes(id) ? cur.filter((x) => x !== id) : [...cur, id] });
   170	                };
   171	                return (
   172	                    <FormItem className="space-y-3 rounded-2xl border border-ant-line bg-ant-paper p-5">
   173	                        <div className="space-y-0.5">
   174	                            <FormLabel className="font-display text-[14px] font-semibold text-ant-ink block">{title}</FormLabel>
   175	                            <p className="font-serif italic text-[12px] text-ant-mid leading-snug">{hint}</p>
   176	                        </div>
   177	                        <div className="flex items-center gap-2">
   178	                            {modes.map(([m, lbl]) => (
   179	                                <button
   180	                                    type="button"
   181	                                    key={m}
   182	                                    onClick={() => setMode(m)}
   183	                                    className={cn(
   184	                                        'h-9 px-3.5 rounded-full text-[11px] font-semibold border transition cursor-pointer',
   185	                                        access.mode === m ? 'bg-ant-ink text-white border-ant-ink shadow-sm'
   186	                                            : 'border-ant-line text-ant-mid hover:text-ant-ink hover:border-ant-ink/30',
   187	                                    )}
   188	                                >
   189	                                    {lbl}
   190	                                </button>
   191	                            ))}
   192	                        </div>
   193	                        {access.mode === 'specific' && (
   194	                            <div className="grid grid-cols-1 gap-1.5 pt-1">
   195	                                {!conns?.length ? (
   196	                                    <p className="font-serif italic text-[12px] text-ant-mid">{emptyText}</p>
   197	                                ) : (
   198	                                    conns.map((c: any) => {
   199	                                        const on = (access.ids || []).includes(c.id);
   200	                                        return (
   201	                                            <button
   202	                                                type="button"
   203	                                                key={c.id}
   204	                                                onClick={() => toggle(c.id)}
   205	                                                className={cn('flex items-center gap-2.5 p-2.5 rounded-xl border text-left transition cursor-pointer',
   206	                                                    on ? 'border-ant-orange bg-ant-orange/5' : 'border-ant-line hover:border-ant-ink/20')}
   207	                                            >
   208	                                                <span className={cn('w-4 h-4 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shrink-0 border',
   209	                                                    on ? 'bg-ant-orange border-ant-orange text-white' : 'border-ant-line')}>
   210	                                                    {on && <Check className="w-3 h-3" />}
   211	                                                </span>
   212	                                                <span className="text-[12px] text-ant-ink truncate">
   213	                                                    {c[primaryKey] || c[secondaryKey]} <span className="text-ant-mid">· {c[secondaryKey]}</span>
   214	                                                </span>
   215	                                            </button>
   216	                                        );
   217	                                    })
   218	                                )}
   219	                            </div>
   220	                        )}
   221	                    </FormItem>

   230	Deno.serve(async (req) => {
   231	  if (req.method === "OPTIONS") {
   232	    return new Response("ok", { headers: corsHeaders });
   233	  }
   234	
   235	  try {
   236	    const supabaseClient = createClient(
   237	      Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_URL") ?? "",
   238	      Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY") ?? "",
   239	      {
   240	        auth: {
   241	          autoRefreshToken: false,
   242	          persistSession: false,
   243	        },
   244	      },
   245	    );
   246	
   247	    // ── Caller authorization (complete mediation) ─────────────────────────
   248	    // The router now requires a valid JWT to reach this function, but any
   249	    // authenticated user of any role would pass that gate. Because this
   250	    // function runs on the service_role key (above RLS/column policies), it
   251	    // must authorize the caller itself: resolve the bearer to a user, then
   252	    // require an admin role from the DB (not from JWT metadata).
   253	    const jwt = (req.headers.get("Authorization") ?? "").replace(/^Bearer\s+/i, "");
   254	    if (!jwt) return json({ error: "unauthorized" }, 401);
   255	    const caller = createClient(
   256	      Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_URL") ?? "",
   257	      Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_ANON_KEY") ?? "",
   258	      { global: { headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${jwt}` } }, auth: { persistSession: false } },
   259	    );
   260	    // getUser(jwt) — explicit token. The no-arg form needs a client session, which
   261	    // this stateless service client never has, so it 401'd every call.
   262	    const { data: { user: callerUser }, error: callerErr } = await caller.auth.getUser(jwt);
   263	    if (callerErr || !callerUser) return json({ error: "unauthorized" }, 401);

   265	      .from("users")

   278	      const { email, password, name, username, role, whatsapp_access, ga_access, meta_access, backup_admin, backup_root: _backupRootIgnored, ...rest } = userData;
   279	      // A BO caller creating a "business owner" is structurally same-business
   280	      // already (the tenant-force below pins `rest["DigiCom Business"]` to
   281	      // me["DigiCom Business"]); only super admin / admin callers are blocked.
   282	      if (role === "business owner" && me.role !== "owner" && me.role !== "business owner") {
   283	        return json({ error: BIZ_OWNER_PROTECTED_MSG }, 403);

   285	      // The PLATFORM 'owner' role can never be minted through this API by a
   286	      // non-owner caller — a BO promoting a peer to platform owner would be a
   287	      // full privilege escalation (mirrors the DB trigger's boundary guard).
   288	      if (role === "owner" && me.role !== "owner") {
   289	        return json({ error: "Seule la plateforme peut gérer le rôle propriétaire de la plateforme." }, 403);

   317	        }])

   325	        if (delErr) {

   354	      // Business moves are PLATFORM-OWNER-ONLY. Without this strip, a BO could
   355	      // relocate himself (or a peer) into another business through this

   358	      if (me.role !== "owner") {

   368	      const { data: targetUser } = await supabaseClient

   372	      }

   374	      if (me.role !== "owner") {

   376	        // platform 'owner' role — mirrors the DB trigger's boundary guard.
   377	        if (rest.role === "owner") {
   378	          return json({ error: "Seule la plateforme peut gérer le rôle propriétaire de la plateforme." }, 403);
   379	        }
   380	        if (targetUser?.role === "owner") {
   381	          return json({ error: "Seule la plateforme peut gérer le rôle propriétaire de la plateforme." }, 403);
   382	        }
   383	        const sameBiz = me.role === "business owner" &&
   384	          targetUser?.["DigiCom Business"] === me["DigiCom Business"];
   385	        if ((targetUser?.role === "business owner" || rest.role === "business owner") && !sameBiz) {
   386	          return json({ error: BIZ_OWNER_PROTECTED_MSG }, 403);
   387	        }
   388	        // Last-BO rule: a business must never lose its last business owner.
   389	        // Platform-owner callers bypass this (handled by the `me.role !== "owner"` guard above).
   390	        if (targetUser?.role === "business owner" && rest.role && rest.role !== "business owner") {
   391	          const { count } = await supabaseClient
   392	            .from("users")
   393	            .select("id", { count: "exact", head: true })
   394	            .eq("role", "business owner")
   395	            .eq("DigiCom Business", targetUser["DigiCom Business"])
   396	            .neq("id", userId);
   397	          if (!count) {
   398	            return json({ error: LAST_BIZ_OWNER_MSG }, 403);
   399	          }
   400	        }
   401	      }
   402	
   403	      // Read the current auth login email so we only touch GoTrue when something
   404	      // actually changed. Forwarding an unchanged email made GoTrue treat every
   405	      // edit (even password-only) as an email change and reject it with 422.
   406	      let currentAuthEmail: string | null = null;
   407	      {
   408	        const { data: cur, error: curErr } = await supabaseClient.auth.admin.getUserById(userId);
   409	        if (curErr) throw friendlyError(curErr);
   410	        currentAuthEmail = cur?.user?.email ?? null;
   411	      }
   412	
   413	      const submittedEmail = typeof email === "string" ? email.trim() : "";
   414	      const emailChanged = submittedEmail.length > 0 &&
   415	        submittedEmail.toLowerCase() !== (currentAuthEmail ?? "").toLowerCase();
   416	
   417	      const authUpdates: Record<string, unknown> = {};
   418	      if (emailChanged) {
   419	        authUpdates.email = submittedEmail;
   420	        authUpdates.email_confirm = true; // admin-initiated change, no confirmation round-trip
   421	      }
   422	      if (password) authUpdates.password = password;
   423	
   424	      if (Object.keys(authUpdates).length > 0) {
   425	        const { error: authError } = await supabaseClient.auth.admin.updateUserById(
   426	          userId,
   427	          authUpdates,
   428	        );
   429	        if (authError) throw friendlyError(authError, emailChanged ? "email" : "password");
   430	      }
   431	
   432	      // auth.users is the source of truth for the login email; the profile email
   433	      // always mirrors it. This both prevents and self-heals drift on every edit.
   434	      const canonicalEmail = emailChanged ? submittedEmail : currentAuthEmail;
   435	      const profilePatch: Record<string, unknown> = { ...rest };
   436	      if (canonicalEmail) profilePatch.email = canonicalEmail;
   437	
   438	      const { data: profileData, error: profileError } = await supabaseClient
   439	        .from("users")
   440	        .update(profilePatch)
   441	        .eq("id", userId)
   442	        .select()
   443	        .single();
   444	
   445	      if (profileError) throw friendlyError(profileError);
   446	
   447	      try {
   448	        await applyWhatsappAccess(supabaseClient, userId, whatsapp_access);
   449	      } catch (waErr) {
   450	        console.error("applyWhatsappAccess (update) failed:", waErr);
   451	      }
   452	      try {
   453	        await applyGaAccess(supabaseClient, userId, ga_access);
   454	        await applyMetaAccess(supabaseClient, userId, meta_access);
   455	      } catch (accErr) {
   456	        console.error("applyGa/MetaAccess (update) failed:", accErr);
   457	      }
   458	
   459	      return new Response(JSON.stringify(profileData), {
   460	        headers: { ...corsHeaders, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
   461	        status: 200,
   462	      });
   463	    }
   464	
   465	    if (action === "delete") {
   466	      // Capture identity BEFORE deletion so the notice can be emailed afterwards.
   467	      const { data: doomed } = await supabaseClient
   468	        .from("users").select('name, email, role, "DigiCom Business"').eq("id", userId).maybeSingle();
   469	
   470	      if (doomed?.role === "business owner") {
   471	        const sameBiz = me.role === "business owner" &&
   472	          doomed["DigiCom Business"] === me["DigiCom Business"];
   473	        if (me.role !== "owner" && !sameBiz) {
   474	          return json({ error: BIZ_OWNER_PROTECTED_MSG }, 403);
   475	        }
   476	        // Last-BO rule: a business must never lose its last business owner.
   477	        // Platform-owner callers bypass this (they may orphan deliberately).
   478	        if (me.role !== "owner") {
   479	          const { count } = await supabaseClient
   480	            .from("users")
   481	            .select("id", { count: "exact", head: true })
   482	            .eq("role", "business owner")
   483	            .eq("DigiCom Business", doomed["DigiCom Business"])
   484	            .neq("id", userId);
   485	          if (!count) {
   486	            return json({ error: LAST_BIZ_OWNER_MSG }, 403);
   487	          }
   488	        }
   489	      }
   490	
   491	      // Eligibility guard: hard delete is only for users with zero operational
   492	      // references (mirrors public.user_deletion_profile's hard_deletable rule).
   493	      // Can't call that RPC here — it's SECDEF-gated on auth.uid(), which is
   494	      // NULL under this service-role client — so the same count set runs directly.
   495	      const { counts, total } = await countUserReferences(supabaseClient, userId);
   496	      if (total > 0) {
   497	        return json({
   498	          error: "Cet utilisateur a un historique d'activité — archivez-le au lieu de le supprimer.",
   499	          total,
   500	          counts,
   501	        }, 403);
   502	      }
   503	
   504	      // Public row first, then auth: if auth cleanup fails after the profile is
   505	      // gone, that's an orphaned auth row (safe — no app-visible profile). The
   506	      // reverse order (previous behavior) could leave an orphaned PROFILE row
   507	      // pointing at a deleted auth user, which is the reported bug.
   508	      const { error: profileError } = await supabaseClient
   509	        .from("users")
   510	        .delete()
   511	        .eq("id", userId);
   512	
   513	      if (profileError) throw profileError;
   514	
   515	      const { error: authError } = await supabaseClient.auth.admin.deleteUser(userId);
   516	      if (authError) {
   517	        console.error("delete: auth cleanup failed after public delete succeeded — partial deletion", userId, authError);
   518	        return json({ error: "Suppression partielle — contactez la plateforme.", partial: true }, 500);
   519	      }
   520	
   521	      // Best-effort "Compte supprimé" email — never affects the deletion result.
   522	      try {
   523	        if (doomed?.email) {
   524	          const ROLE_FR: Record<string, string> = {
   525	            "super admin": "Super administrateur", "business owner": "Propriétaire d'entreprise", "admin": "Administrateur", "commercial": "Commercial",
   526	            "courier": "Livreur", "mediabuyer": "Media buyer", "owner": "Propriétaire",
   527	          };
   528	          let businessName = "votre entreprise";
   529	          if (doomed["DigiCom Business"]) {
   530	            const { data: biz } = await supabaseClient.from("businesses").select("name").eq("id", doomed["DigiCom Business"]).maybeSingle();
   531	            if (biz?.name) businessName = biz.name;
   532	          }
   533	          const nowFr = (() => {
   534	            try { return new Date().toLocaleString("fr-FR", { timeZone: "Africa/Casablanca", dateStyle: "long", timeStyle: "short" }); }
   535	            catch { return new Date().toISOString(); }
   536	          })();
   537	          const SERVICE = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY") ?? "";
   538	          await fetch(`${Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_URL")}/functions/v1/send-email`, {
   539	            method: "POST",
   540	            headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json", "Authorization": `Bearer ${SERVICE}`, "apikey": SERVICE },
   541	            body: JSON.stringify({
   542	              key: "account_deleted", to: doomed.email,
   543	              vars: {
   544	                user_name: doomed.name || "",
   545	                role_label: ROLE_FR[doomed.role] || String(doomed.role || ""),
   546	                business_name: businessName,
   547	                deleted_at: nowFr,
   548	              },
   549	            }),
   550	          });
   551	        }
   552	      } catch (e) {
   553	        console.error("account_deleted email failed:", e);
   554	      }
   555	
   556	      return new Response(JSON.stringify({ success: true }), {
   557	        headers: { ...corsHeaders, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
   558	        status: 200,
   559	      });
   560	    }
   561	
   562	    if (action === "archive") {
   563	      const { data: target } = await supabaseClient
   564	        .from("users").select('role, "DigiCom Business", archived_at').eq("id", userId).maybeSingle();
   565	      if (!target) return json({ error: "Utilisateur introuvable." }, 404);
   566	
   567	      // Platform-owner boundary: only the platform can archive a platform owner.
   568	      if (target.role === "owner" && me.role !== "owner") {
   569	        return json({ error: "Seule la plateforme peut gérer le rôle propriétaire de la plateforme." }, 403);
   570	      }
   571	
   572	      if (target.role === "business owner") {
   573	        const sameBiz = me.role === "business owner" &&
   574	          target["DigiCom Business"] === me["DigiCom Business"];
   575	        if (me.role !== "owner" && !sameBiz) {
   576	          return json({ error: BIZ_OWNER_PROTECTED_MSG }, 403);
   577	        }
   578	        // Last-BO rule: archiving a business owner requires another ACTIVE,
   579	        // non-archived business owner left in the same business.
   580	        if (me.role !== "owner") {
   581	          const { count } = await supabaseClient
   582	            .from("users")
   583	            .select("id", { count: "exact", head: true })
   584	            .eq("role", "business owner")
   585	            .eq("DigiCom Business", target["DigiCom Business"])
   586	            .eq("active", true)
   587	            .is("archived_at", null)
   588	            .neq("id", userId);
   589	          if (!count) {
   590	            return json({ error: LAST_BIZ_OWNER_MSG }, 403);
   591	          }
   592	        }
   593	      }
   594	
   595	      const { error: profileError } = await supabaseClient
   596	        .from("users")
   597	        .update({ active: false, archived_at: new Date().toISOString() })
   598	        .eq("id", userId);
   599	      if (profileError) throw profileError;
   600	
   601	      // Soft delete (second arg true): kills sessions + blocks login, keeps the
   602	      // auth row and reserves the email. If it fails after the public archive
   603	      // already succeeded, no revert is needed — active=false already blocks
   604	      // the app via the existing suspension handling, so it's fail-safe.
   605	      const { error: authError } = await supabaseClient.auth.admin.deleteUser(userId, true);
   606	      if (authError) {
   607	        console.error("archive: soft auth delete failed after public archive succeeded", userId, authError);
   608	        return json({ success: true, auth_soft_delete: false });
   609	      }
   610	
   611	      return json({ success: true });
   612	    }
   613	
   614	    if (action === "set_password") {
   615	      const { user_id, new_password } = body;
   616	      if (typeof new_password !== "string" || new_password.length < 8) {
   617	        return json({ error: "Mot de passe trop court (8 caractères minimum)." }, 400);
   618	      }
   619	      const { data: targetUser } = await supabaseClient
   620	        .from("users").select('"DigiCom Business", archived_at').eq("id", user_id).maybeSingle();
   621	      if (targetUser?.archived_at) {
   622	        return json({ error: "Utilisateur archivé — aucune modification possible." }, 403);
   623	      }
   624	      if (me.role !== "owner") {
   625	        const sameBiz = me.role === "business owner" &&
   626	          targetUser?.["DigiCom Business"] === me["DigiCom Business"];
   627	        if (!sameBiz) return json({ error: "forbidden" }, 403);
   628	      }
   629	
   630	      const { error: pwError } = await supabaseClient.auth.admin.updateUserById(user_id, { password: new_password });
   631	      if (pwError) throw friendlyError(pwError, "password");
   632	
   633	      return json({ success: true });
   634	    }
   635	
   636	    return new Response(JSON.stringify({ error: "Invalid action" }), {
   637	      headers: { ...corsHeaders, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
   638	      status: 400,
   639	    });
   640	  } catch (error) {
   641	    return new Response(JSON.stringify({ error: (error as Error).message }), {
   642	      headers: { ...corsHeaders, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
   643	      status: 400,
   644	    });
   645	  }
   646	});
     1	// @ts-nocheck
     2	// Admin-gated: activate / deactivate a user account, then email account_reactivated /
     3	// account_suspended (with the admin-supplied reason). The status flip is the primary action;
     4	// the email is best-effort (a mail failure won't undo the flip). Caller must be a super admin /
     5	// admin in the SAME business as the target user.
     6	import "jsr:@supabase/functions-js/edge-runtime.d.ts";
     7	import { createClient } from "https://esm.sh/@supabase/supabase-js@2.39.3";
     8	
     9	const corsHeaders = {
    10	  "Access-Control-Allow-Origin": "*",
    11	  "Access-Control-Allow-Headers": "authorization, x-client-info, apikey, content-type",
    12	};
    13	const json = (o: unknown, s: number) =>
    14	  new Response(JSON.stringify(o), { headers: { ...corsHeaders, "Content-Type": "application/json" }, status: s });
    15	
    16	const ROLE_FR: Record<string, string> = {
    17	  "super admin": "Super administrateur", "business owner": "Propriétaire d'entreprise", "admin": "Administrateur", "commercial": "Commercial",
    18	  "courier": "Livreur", "mediabuyer": "Media buyer", "owner": "Propriétaire",
    19	};
    20	const BIZ_OWNER_PROTECTED_MSG = "Le propriétaire de l'entreprise ne peut être géré que par un propriétaire ou la plateforme.";
    21	
    22	Deno.serve(async (req) => {
    23	  if (req.method === "OPTIONS") return new Response("ok", { headers: corsHeaders });
    24	
    25	  try {
    26	    const url = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_URL") ?? "";
    27	    const SERVICE = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY") ?? "";
    28	    const service = createClient(url, SERVICE, { auth: { persistSession: false, autoRefreshToken: false } });
    29	
    30	    const jwt = (req.headers.get("Authorization") ?? "").replace(/^bearer\s+/i, "").trim();
    31	    const { data: { user }, error: userErr } = await service.auth.getUser(jwt);
    32	    if (userErr || !user) return json({ ok: false, reason: "not_authenticated" }, 401);
    33	
    34	    const { data: me } = await service.from("users").select('role, "DigiCom Business", active').eq("id", user.id).single();
    35	    if (!me) return json({ ok: false, reason: "profile_not_found" }, 403);
    36	    if (me.active === false) return json({ ok: false, reason: "account_disabled" }, 403);
    37	    if (!["super admin", "business owner", "admin"].includes(String(me.role))) return json({ ok: false, reason: "forbidden" }, 403);
    38	    const businessId = me["DigiCom Business"];
    39	    if (!businessId) return json({ ok: false, reason: "no_business" }, 400);
    40	
    41	    const body = await req.json().catch(() => ({}));
    42	    const userId = String(body?.user_id ?? "");
    43	    if (!userId) return json({ ok: false, reason: "missing_user" }, 400);
    44	    const active = body?.active === true;
    45	    let reason = String(body?.reason ?? "").trim();
    46	    const origin = typeof body?.origin === "string" && /^https?:\/\//.test(body.origin)
    47	      ? body.origin.replace(/\/$/, "") : (Deno.env.get("SITE_URL") || "https://igrowfast.com").replace(/\/$/, "");
    48	
    49	    // Target must belong to the caller's business.
    50	    const { data: target } = await service.from("users")
    51	      .select('id, name, email, role, "DigiCom Business", archived_at').eq("id", userId).maybeSingle();
    52	    if (!target) return json({ ok: false, reason: "not_found" }, 404);
    53	    if (target["DigiCom Business"] !== businessId) return json({ ok: false, reason: "forbidden" }, 403);
    54	    if (userId === user.id) return json({ ok: false, reason: "cannot_change_self" }, 400);
    55	    if (target.archived_at) {
    56	      return json({ ok: false, reason: "archived", error: "Utilisateur archivé — aucune modification possible." }, 403);
    57	    }
    58	    // Target already verified same-business above. No last-BO check —
    59	    // suspension is reversible.
    60	    if (target.role === "business owner" && me.role !== "owner" && me.role !== "business owner") {
    61	      return json({ ok: false, reason: "forbidden", error: BIZ_OWNER_PROTECTED_MSG }, 403);
    62	    }
    63	
    64	    // Primary action: flip the status (mirrors the previous direct update, now server-side).
    65	    const { error: updErr } = await service.from("users").update({ active }).eq("id", userId);
    66	    if (updErr) return json({ ok: false, reason: "update_failed", detail: updErr.message }, 400);
    67	
    68	    // Best-effort notification.
    69	    let emailWarning: string | null = null;
    70	    if (target.email) {
    71	      const { data: biz } = await service.from("businesses").select("name").eq("id", businessId).maybeSingle();
    72	      const nowFr = (() => {
    73	        try { return new Date().toLocaleString("fr-FR", { timeZone: "Africa/Casablanca", dateStyle: "long", timeStyle: "short" }); }
    74	        catch { return new Date().toISOString(); }
    75	      })();
    76	      if (!reason) reason = active ? "Votre accès a été rétabli par l'administrateur." : "Non précisé.";
    77	      const key = active ? "account_reactivated" : "account_suspended";
    78	      const vars: Record<string, unknown> = {
    79	        user_name: target.name || "",
    80	        role_label: ROLE_FR[target.role] || String(target.role),
    81	        business_name: biz?.name || "votre entreprise",
    82	        reason,
    83	      };
    84	      if (active) { vars.reactivated_at = nowFr; vars.login_url = `${origin}/auth`; }
    85	      else { vars.suspended_at = nowFr; }
    86	
    87	      try {
    88	        const resp = await fetch(`${url}/functions/v1/send-email`, {
    89	          method: "POST",
    90	          headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json", "Authorization": `Bearer ${SERVICE}`, "apikey": SERVICE },
    91	          body: JSON.stringify({ key, to: target.email, user_id: userId, vars }),
    92	        });
    93	        const out = await resp.json().catch(() => ({}));
    94	        if (!resp.ok || !out?.ok) emailWarning = out?.detail || out?.error || `status ${resp.status}`;
    95	      } catch (e) {
    96	        emailWarning = (e as Error).message;
    97	      }
    98	    }
    99	
   100	    return json({ ok: true, active, email_warning: emailWarning }, 200);
   101	  } catch (e) {
   102	    return json({ ok: false, reason: "error", detail: (e as Error).message }, 500);
   103	  }
   104	});
     1	import React from 'react';
     2	import { useQuery } from '@tanstack/react-query';
     3	import { AlertTriangle, Archive, Loader2, Trash2 } from 'lucide-react';
     4	import { supabase } from '@/lib/supabase';
     5	import { Button } from '@/components/ui/button';
     6	import {
     7	    Dialog,
     8	    DialogContent,
     9	    DialogDescription,
    10	    DialogFooter,
    11	    DialogHeader,
    12	    DialogTitle,
    13	} from '@/components/ui/dialog';
    14	import { Skeleton } from '@/components/ui/skeleton';
    15	import { cn } from '@/lib/utils';
    16	
    17	/**
    18	 * Adaptive suppression dialog for the Users console.
    19	 *
    20	 * The server decides the face: `user_deletion_profile` (mig 20260730100000)
    21	 * counts the target's MEANINGFUL operational/financial references and returns
    22	 * `hard_deletable`. Zero references → the destructive face (real delete).
    23	 * Otherwise → the ARCHIVE face with receipts: load-bearing users are never
    24	 * hard-deleted (the payment ledgers cascade, so a "successful" delete would
    25	 * silently destroy money history).
    26	 */
    27	type DeletionProfile = {
    28	    commandes: number;
    29	    paiements: number;
    30	    colis: number;
    31	    notes: number;
    32	    campagnes: number;
    33	    demandes: number;
    34	    historique: number;
    35	    total: number;
    36	    hard_deletable: boolean;
    37	};
    38	
    39	const RECEIPT_ROWS: Array<{ key: keyof DeletionProfile; label: string }> = [
    40	    { key: 'commandes', label: 'Commandes' },
    41	    { key: 'paiements', label: 'Paiements' },
    42	    { key: 'colis', label: 'Colis' },
    43	    { key: 'notes', label: 'Notes' },
    44	    { key: 'campagnes', label: 'Campagnes' },
    45	    { key: 'demandes', label: 'Demandes' },
    46	    { key: 'historique', label: 'Historique' },
    47	];
    48	
    49	const DIALOG_SHELL =
    50	    'max-w-md p-0 border border-ant-line bg-ant-paper rounded-[20px] shadow-[0_32px_90px_-28px_rgba(20,20,19,0.4)] overflow-hidden max-h-[92vh] flex flex-col';
    51	
    52	export const DeleteOrArchiveUserDialog: React.FC<{
    53	    /** Target row — `null` keeps the dialog closed and the profile query idle. */
    54	    user: any | null;
    55	    onClose: () => void;
    56	    onHardDelete: () => void;
    57	    hardDeletePending: boolean;
    58	    onArchive: () => void;
    59	    archivePending: boolean;
    60	}> = ({ user, onClose, onHardDelete, hardDeletePending, onArchive, archivePending }) => {
    61	    const busy = hardDeletePending || archivePending;
    62	
    63	    const { data: profile, isLoading, error } = useQuery<DeletionProfile>({
    64	        queryKey: ['user-deletion-profile', user?.id],
    65	        enabled: !!user?.id,
    66	        gcTime: 0,          // every re-open re-reads the live reference counts
    67	        retry: false,
    68	        queryFn: async () => {
    69	            // RPC newer than the generated types → untyped rpc cast (house escape hatch).
    70	            // eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any
    71	            const { data, error: rpcError } = await (supabase as any).rpc('user_deletion_profile', {
    72	                p_user_id: user.id,
    73	            });
    74	            if (rpcError) throw new Error(rpcError.message);
    75	            return data as DeletionProfile;
    76	        },
    77	    });
    78	
    79	    const receipts = profile
    80	        ? RECEIPT_ROWS.map((r) => ({ ...r, count: Number(profile[r.key] ?? 0) })).filter((r) => r.count > 0)
    81	        : [];
    82	
    83	    const face: 'loading' | 'error' | 'delete' | 'archive' =
    84	        isLoading || !user ? 'loading'
    85	            : error ? 'error'
    86	                : profile?.hard_deletable ? 'delete'
    87	                    : 'archive';
    88	
    89	    const hairline =
    90	        face === 'archive' ? 'from-ant-orange via-ant-orange/60'
    91	            : face === 'loading' ? 'from-ant-ink via-ant-ink/40'
    92	                : 'from-ant-rose via-ant-rose/60';
    93	
    94	    return (
    95	        <Dialog open={!!user} onOpenChange={(open) => { if (!open && !busy) onClose(); }}>
    96	            <DialogContent className={DIALOG_SHELL}>

   135	                                ) : face === 'archive' ? (

   139	                                ) : (
   140	                                    <>L&rsquo;activité de <span className="not-italic font-semibold text-ant-ink">« {user?.name} »</span> n&rsquo;a pas pu être vérifiée. Aucune action n&rsquo;a été effectuée.</>
   141	                                )}
   142	                            </DialogDescription>
   143	                        </div>
   144	                    </div>
   145	                </DialogHeader>
   146	
   147	                {face === 'loading' && (

   152	                    </div>

   154

   193	                            </div>

   196	                            L&rsquo;archivage désactive définitivement le compte et la connexion. L&rsquo;historique et les
   197	                            attributions restent intacts.
   198	                        </p>
   199	                    </div>
   200	                )}
   201	
   202	                <DialogFooter className="px-6 py-4 border-t border-ant-line gap-2">
   203	                    {face === 'error' ? (

   207	                        >

   217	                            >

   219	                            </Button>
   220	                            {face === 'delete' && (

     1	---
     2	name: user-mgmt-uniqueness-model
     3	description: How user create/edit uniqueness and email sync work in manage-users (post 2026-06-18 fix)
     4	metadata: 
     5	  node_type: memory
     6	  type: project
     7	  originSessionId: b0aa4395-08fa-4308-b1ff-53c03772f604

     9	
    10	User accounts (`public.users` + `auth.users`) are managed only by the `manage-users` edge function (callers: admin/Users.tsx, owner/Users.tsx, owner/Businesses.tsx createAdmin, admin/DeliveryServices.tsx courier; plus a direct bank-field self-update in mediabuyer/Payments.tsx). No DB trigger/RPC creates users; the profile row is inserted explicitly (not via handle_new_user).
    11	
    12	Uniqueness model (set 2026-06-18):
    13	- **username = unique PER BUSINESS, case-insensitive** via index `users_username_per_business_key` on `(COALESCE("DigiCom Business", '0000…0000'::uuid), lower(username))`. Owners (NULL business, `users_owner_no_business_chk`) share the sentinel bucket → unique among themselves. Replaced the old global `users_username_key`.
    14	- **email = GLOBAL unique** (kept `users_email_key`) because Supabase Auth requires one login email per project. Cross-business email reuse is intentionally NOT supported (would need synthetic-email login redesign — user chose against it).
    15	
    16	manage-users invariants:
    17	- `auth.users.email` is the source of truth for the login email; `public.users.email` always mirrors it. The update path reconciles profile→auth email on every edit (self-heals drift).
    18	- The update path only forwards `email` to GoTrue when it actually changed (compares to `auth.admin.getUserById`). **Why:** previously it always sent email; when profile/auth emails had drifted, GoTrue read it as an email change and 422'd, aborting before the password update — so password-only edits silently failed. **How to apply:** never reintroduce unconditional `authUpdates.email = email`.
    19	- Errors are mapped to human messages via `friendlyError` (username taken in business / email used platform-wide). GoTrue `AuthApiError` can be opaque (`.message` == "{}", no `.code`), so `friendlyError` takes a `context` ('email'|'password'|'create') to stay actionable.
    20	- Frontend must read the edge error body via `src/lib/edgeError.ts::edgeFnErrorMessage(error,data)` — `supabase.functions.invoke` hides it on `error.context` (a Response), not `data`; otherwise the UI shows the useless "Edge Function returned a non-2xx status code". Wired into all manage-users callers.
    21	- Password policy is a GoTrue project setting: requires uppercase+lowercase+digit (min 6); leaked-password protection OFF. A weak password → 422 → 400, NOT a bug in the fix. Change it in Dashboard → Authentication → Password settings (no SQL/MCP tool for it).
    22	
    23	Deployed live = committed file; redeploy via MCP `deploy_edge_function` keeps verify_jwt=false. Note open security gap: manage-users does no caller-role check (service-role + verify_jwt off) — any authenticated caller can invoke it. Related: [[orders-parcels-logistics-mirror]] (same "live fn drifts from migrations" caution).
    24	
    25	
    26	## Archived index-line detail (moved from MEMORY.md 2026-07-03)
    27	username unique PER BUSINESS (case-insensitive, owners share sentinel bucket); email GLOBAL unique (auth constraint); manage-users only sends email to GoTrue when changed, profile email mirrors auth (don't reintroduce unconditional email forward → password-only 422)

    10	Standardized self-profile system (built 2026-06-20). One pattern for all 5 roles.
    11	
    12	**Avatar self-edit** — `src/lib/profile.ts`: `saveOwnAvatar({config|dataUrl})` (uploaded photo → `avatar` bucket path `self/<uid>.png`, sets avatar_url with `?v=` cache-bust; generated → avatar_config; clears the other). `avatar` bucket has PUBLIC insert/update policies ("Anyone can upload an avatar") so any role uploads client-side, no edge fn. `saveOwnBasicInfo({name,username,phone})`. `AvatarEditDialog` (dark) reuses `AvatarStudio`.
    13	
    14	**OTP credential change (no link, logged-in)** — `src/components/profile/SecuritySection.tsx` + `src/lib/selfSecurity.ts`. Two authenticated edge fns (verify_jwt, identify user via `service.auth.getUser(jwt)`):
    15	- `self-credential-otp`: inserts a `credential_change_requests` row keyed to the JWT user with a RANDOM internal token_hash (link token unused for self-flow) + otp_hash, emails `otp_code` to CURRENT email.
    16	- `self-credential-confirm`: verifies latest unconsumed OTP row for the user. With `new_email`/`new_password` → applies via `auth.admin.updateUserById` + mirrors users.email + confirmation emails (`password_changed`/`email_changed`). With NEITHER → just verifies (this is the email-2FA login gate). Reuses the proven send-email machine [[email-templates-system]].
    17	
    18	**2FA — TOTP ONLY** (email-OTP 2FA was built then REMOVED per user 2026-06-20: `users.mfa_email_enabled` column dropped, `setEmail2fa` + the SecuritySection email toggle + the Auth email branch all deleted; the session-withholding re-architecture proposal was dropped too). TOTP via Supabase NATIVE MFA (`auth.mfa.enroll/challenge/verify/listFactors/unenroll`), enrolled via SecuritySection QR dialog. `enrollTotp` FIRST unenrolls any stale UNVERIFIED totp factor + enrolls with friendlyName 'Authenticator' (else GoTrue 422 "factor with friendly name '' already exists" → stuck QR spinner). NOTE: still only client-gated (Auth.tsx requires the TOTP challenge before navigating, but RLS does not yet require aal2 — to make it truly enforced add the documented restrictive `auth.jwt()->>'aal'='aal2'` policy; not done, per "no auth problems").
    19	
    20	**Auth gate** — `Auth.tsx` after `signInWithPassword`: `mfa.getAuthenticatorAssuranceLevel()` → if nextLevel aal2 & current aal1 → TOTP challenge view (`challengeAndVerify`); else `proceed()`. Cancel → `signOut`. `pending2FA` state drives a 3rd card view alongside login/forgot. The credential-change OTP (self-credential-otp/confirm) stays — that's for in-profile email/password change, separate from 2FA.
    21	
    22	**Pages** — `ProfileScaffold` (shared: hero+editable avatar, editable identity, SecuritySection) backs NEW `owner/Profile` + `mediabuyer/Profile` (routes `/owner/profile`, `/mediabuyer/profile`; sidebar cards now NavLinks). admin/seller/courier keep their own layouts but all dropped `<SecuritySection/>` and clickable-avatar (`AvatarEditDialog`). admin's old direct `auth.updateUser` password section REMOVED (now OTP-gated). Fixed: admin avatar was uploading to wrong `providers-logos` bucket → `avatar`.
    23	
    24	Runtime-verified: build green, edge-fn auth gate (401 unauthenticated), no new advisors. NOT yet browser-tested end-to-end (TOTP enroll, email-2FA login, OTP apply) — needs a real session.

    12	# Business owner role (TKT-10 / Notion Ref 9, shipped 2026-07-29, commit 4b0d033)
    13	
    14	## TKT-10b UPDATE (2026-07-29 evening, commit 8027b7c): MULTIPLICITY + management powers
    15	- Model CHANGED same day: MULTIPLE business owners per business (unique index DROPPED; one-per-biz rows below are historical). BOs manage users INCL. other BOs within their OWN business: direct create (typed password, email_confirm, no email round-trip — manage-users create), invite (BO tile, BO/owner callers only), promote/demote any tenant role, set_password (new action, top-level {user_id,new_password}), delete, suspend.
    16	- Trigger v3 invariants (all live-battery-proven): platform-'owner' boundary (nobody but platform owner mints/strips one — closes BO→owner escalation), business-move guard (BO relocation = platform-owner-only; edge strips "DigiCom Business" from update payloads for non-owner callers — service-role writes bypass the trigger so the EDGE is the tenant-move enforcer), last-BO rule serialized via businesses-row FOR UPDATE (closes READ COMMITTED race).
    17	- Cross-biz writes die at RLS (0 rows) BEFORE the trigger — layered fail-closed.
    18	- Residuals: edge last-BO count is unserialized (service-role TOCTOU, ms window, accepted); platform owner can't use create-invitation/account-status-change (no business — his path is manage-users, pre-existing).
    19	- UI: Users.tsx viewer-role query (keyed by uid) gates BO powers; DirectCreateUserDialog/ChangeRoleDialog/SetPasswordDialog in src/components/users/.
    20	- TKT-10c (0dff829): DirectCreateUserDialog carries per-role config sections (wizard buildPayload contract EXACTLY, spread top-level in userData); UserModal opens to BO for ALL roles (identity section, courier tab, in-modal role switch resets config; non-BO byte-identical). TRAP: manage-users applyGa/MetaAccess silently no-op WITHOUT business_id inside ga_access/meta_access — consume-invitation injects it server-side, manage-users does NOT; any direct caller must include it. whatsapp_access still offered in NO UI (API-only, flagged to owner).
    21	
    22	**Model (original 2026-07-29 morning, superseded above):** new `user_role` enum value `business owner` — NOT the pointer design. Full super-admin parity everywhere + extra privileges to be layered later. Assigned ONLY at business creation (Businesses.tsx createAdminMutation); NOT invitable (create-invitation 403s the target), NOT assignable via user-edit (manage-users guards + z.enum excludes). Max one per business: partial unique index `users_one_business_owner_per_biz` + CHECK `users_business_owner_needs_business` (no NULL-biz owner). Backfill: 7 earliest super admins promoted (owner-approved list; Golden=Oualid f8df86f8).
    23	
    24	**Parity surface (migrations 20260729140000 enum / 150000 parity / 160000 backfill):** 44 RLS policies (check_user_role ARRAY pattern, regenerated from live catalog), `current_user_is_business_admin()`, 16 DB fns, `whatsapp.profiles` view (BOTH role arrays; security_barrier=true preserved via WITH clause), 14 edge fns (+ FR label `Propriétaire d'entreprise`), SPA guards + ROLE_META badge (Gem, own bucket) + WA auth adapter bucket.
    25	
    26	**Protection (defense-in-depth, all live-proven):**
    27	1. Table ACL: `authenticated` has NO DML grants on public.users (pre-existing hardening) — direct PostgREST writes die at permission-denied.
    28	2. Trigger `trg_protect_business_owner` (BEFORE INSERT|UPDATE|DELETE, fast-path short-circuit): demote/delete AND promote-into both require actor role='owner'; auth.uid() NULL (service role) passes — edge fns enforce caller checks there (manage-users create/update/delete + account-status-change suspend: caller must be platform owner).
    29	3. Edge guards close self-promotion (rest.role check in update path).
    30	
    31	**TRAPS:**
    32	- **Apply as supabase_admin**: some fns (chat_customer_orders_count) are OWNED by supabase_admin — postgres can't CREATE OR REPLACE them ("must be owner"). Parity migration applied via `docker exec -i supabase-db psql -U supabase_admin`. CREATE OR REPLACE preserves original owners; fns CREATED by supabase_admin are owned by it (protect_business_owner_role is — SECDEF-as-superuser, acceptable, it only reads users+raises).
    33	- **Pipeline exit-code masking incident**: `db.sh --write | tail` in a `&&` chain — the pipe made a FAILED migration look successful and the next step (backfill) ran anyway → 7 users briefly role-flipped without parity (~2 min degradation). NEVER pipe db.sh in a && chain without pipefail.
    34	- **Testing trigger raise paths**: authenticated can't UPDATE users (ACL) — use a transaction-scoped `GRANT UPDATE ... TO authenticated` inside BEGIN…ROLLBACK (DDL is transactional; verified no leak).
    35	- account-status-change is fail-closed for business owners (owner isn't in its caller set — the owner path is manage-users).
    36	- Cosmetic residuals: Users.tsx dropdown shows actions that 403 server-side; analytics UsersSection color map lacks the role (safe fallback).
    37

     1	import { supabase } from '@/lib/supabase';
     2	
     3	function dataURLtoBlob(dataUrl: string): Blob {
     4	    const [head, b64] = dataUrl.split(',');
     5	    const mime = /:(.*?);/.exec(head)?.[1] || 'image/png';
     6	    const bin = atob(b64);
     7	    const arr = new Uint8Array(bin.length);
     8	    for (let i = 0; i < bin.length; i++) arr[i] = bin.charCodeAt(i);
     9	    return new Blob([arr], { type: mime });
    10	}
    11	
    12	/**
    13	 * Save the logged-in user's own avatar. Uploaded photo → `avatar` bucket (avatar_url);
    14	 * generated avatar → avatar_config. The other is cleared so UserAvatar shows the new one.
    15	 */
    16	export async function saveOwnAvatar(v: { config?: object | null; dataUrl?: string | null }): Promise<void> {
    17	    const { data: { user } } = await supabase.auth.getUser();
    18	    if (!user) throw new Error('Not authenticated');
    19	
    20	    if (v.dataUrl) {
    21	        const path = `self/${user.id}.png`;
    22	        const { error: upErr } = await supabase.storage.from('avatar')
    23	            .upload(path, dataURLtoBlob(v.dataUrl), { upsert: true, contentType: 'image/png' });
    24	        if (upErr) throw upErr;
    25	        const base = supabase.storage.from('avatar').getPublicUrl(path).data.publicUrl;
    26	        const url = `${base}?v=${Date.now()}`; // cache-bust the stable path
    27	        const { error } = await supabase.from('users')
    28	            .update({ avatar_url: url, avatar_config: null } as never).eq('id', user.id);
    29	        if (error) throw error;
    30	    } else if (v.config) {
    31	        const { error } = await supabase.from('users')
    32	            .update({ avatar_config: v.config, avatar_url: null } as never).eq('id', user.id);
    33	        if (error) throw error;
    34	    }
    35	}
    36	
    37	/** Save the logged-in user's own basic identity fields. */
    38	export async function saveOwnBasicInfo(patch: { name?: string; username?: string; phone?: string }): Promise<void> {
    39	    const { data: { user } } = await supabase.auth.getUser();
    40	    if (!user) throw new Error('Not authenticated');
    41	    const { error } = await supabase.from('users').update(patch as never).eq('id', user.id);
    42	    if (error) throw error;
    43	}
     1	import React, { useEffect, useMemo, useState } from 'react';
     2	import { useMutation, useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
     3	import { supabase } from '@/lib/supabase';
     4	import { useToast } from '@/hooks/use-toast';

     8	type BankProfile = {
     9	    bank_name?: string | null;
    10	    bank_account_holder?: string | null;
    11	    bank_rib?: string | null;
    12	    cin_number?: string | null;
